He was proud. Of course, proud. He understood well why they went to Gangnam. He could not deny the value of what they protected and the importance of the agreement they upheld.

But still, he was angry.

No matter how noble the cause, what parent would like their child going to a dangerous battlefield? Seeing those guys smiling after surviving such harsh battles made his anger surge uncontrollably.

Hyun Jong quietly bowed his head.

“Everyone... thank you.”

Then everyone was flustered and opened their mouths wide.

“S-sect leader!”

“Ah, no, why are you like this! We were wrong.”

“Beg, you bastards! Beg quickly!”

Hyun Jong, raising his head, shook it at the flustered disciples who didn’t know what to do.

“I’m not thanking you for what you did.”

“...”

“You resolved the crisis in the Central Plains, elevated Mount Hua’s prestige, and showed the entire murim world that the Righteousness still exists.”

“Sect leader...”

Hyun Jong looked at his disciples with sunken eyes.

“But I am not thankful for any of that.”

“...”

“There is only one thing I am thankful to you for.”

He bowed his head once more.

“For returning safe and sound... I truly, truly thank you.”

The disciples of Mount Hua bowed their heads together.

It was clear how much Hyun Jong worried and agonized over them. Looking back, he had only worried about the safety of those fighting inside, never considering the feelings of those left behind. That was probably why he even forgot to send a simple message.

A fresh feeling of guilt arose. The Mount Hua disciples sighed repeatedly.

“Sorry, Sect leader.”

“Sorry.”

“Can we lower our hands now?”

“No.”

“Ugh, really.”

Chung Myung grumbled, but his voice didn’t reach anyone else’s ears.

“Phew.”

When Hyun Jong sighed deeply, Hyun Young beside him clicked his tongue.

“Well, when kids fight wildly, mistakes happen. Don’t be petty about it.”

“What, you brat?”

“Anyway, you get more childish as you age.”

Hyun Sang, watching cautiously nearby, gently stopped Hyun Young.

“Isn’t the sect leader simply worried?”

“Who here isn’t worried about the situation? If the kids came back safely, you should praise them first. That’s what Sect leader should do!”

“Ugh, ugh...”

Hyun Jong had nothing to say and turned his head sharply. Hyun Young clicked his tongue and looked at the kids.

“Next time, think about the person who waits anxiously. I thought the sect leader was going to die.”

“I will keep that in mind, Elder.”

“You definitely will.”

Hyun Young nodded.

“Alright. Now that sect leader seems to have calmed down, tell me what happened.”

Those seated glanced at Baek Cheon, as if telling him to speak on behalf.

Baek Cheon cleared his throat and began to speak.

“First of all...”

The events experienced in the Southern land slowly began to unfold.

“Hmm.”

After hearing the not-so-short story, a groan escaped Hyun Jong’s lips. Everyone looked at him, curious about what he would say first.

“Un Geom.”

“Yes, sect leader.”

“Where are the woman and child you rescued from Hangzhou now?”

Hearing that, smiles naturally appeared on the Mount Hua disciples’ faces. It was so like Hyun Jong.

“The woman and child are resting in a quiet room.”

Un Geom answered calmly and added.

“They have no particular physical abnormalities but are in a very weakened state. Even if we don’t know, for an ordinary woman, going against the water current wouldn’t be easy.”

“That’s right, that’s right.”

“So, I thought resting in a comfortable place was the priority. I asked the Tang family physicians, so there should be no problem.”

“Good. The child is safe too?”

“Yes, Sect leader. You don’t need to worry.”

“That’s a relief.”

Only then did Hyun Jong sigh lightly.

Compared to the rise of the Demonic Cult and the unification of the Four Sects Alliance, two huge events sure to throw the murim world into chaos, the safety of just two survivors might seem trivial.

But Hyun Jong mentioned the safety of those two first among all matters. That revealed what kind of person he was, positively or negatively.

At that moment, Tang Soso quietly spoke.

“The mother and child seem very worried. Being forcibly relocated to a foreign land without relatives...”

“Of course. Naturally so.”

Hyun Jong looked at Hyun Young as if there was no need to think.

“Hyun Young.”

“Yes, sect leader.”

“Make sure they lack nothing where they stay, and find a place for them to settle. Whether it’s Hwa’um, here, or even Sichuan. Help them live comfortably wherever they wish.”

“I will do so, sect leader.”

Hyun Jong nodded with a heavy heart.

That there were survivors and that they were rescued by heavenly luck was certainly good news. But conversely, it meant only two survived in the vast land of Hangzhou.

Of course, some might have fled elsewhere after the incident, so not everyone was sacrificed, but the loss was still great.

“The Demonic Cult...”

The Demonic Cult, as told by them, was far more terrifying and dreadful than he had imagined. Especially considering they had toned down their words out of consideration for Hyun Jong.

“Chung Myung.”

“...”

“Chung Myung.”

After calling twice with no reply, Hyun Jong’s eyebrows twitched.

Hyun Jong’s tightly set mouth and Chung Myung’s pouted lips met in the air.

“...Lower your arm.”

“Yes, sect leader. I obey.”

At the immediate polite reply, Hyun Jong squeezed his eyes shut.

‘That guy, seriously...’

For anyone else, their personality would have changed a dozen times after what they went through these past few days, so how could someone remain so consistent?

At this point, it’s not that his personality is bad, but maybe those trying to fix it are the ones at fault.

“You...”

Hyun Jong, about to say something, sighed deeply. After looking at Chung Myung with a troubled face for a moment, he spoke with strength.

“I won’t ask why you know so much about the Demonic Cult.”

“Ah, that? That is...”

“Enough.”

Just as Chung Myung tried to say something smoothly, Hyun Jong cut him off firmly. It was better not to hear any half-hearted excuses.

“Tell me that later if you feel like it.”

Of course, Hyun Jong was curious about Chung Myung’s story. But relationships don’t get closer by knowing more, nor do they grow distant by knowing less.

Whatever story he had, to him, Chung Myung was just Chung Myung.

There must be a reason why the child who had opened his heart didn’t share his story. What Hyun Jong had to do now was not to push but to wait.

Hyun Jong subtly gestured to others nearby, as if saying their attitude should be no different.

He furrowed his brows slightly and spoke.

“More importantly, since you seem to know the Demonic Cult best among us, I’ll ask. According to Baek Cheon, the Demonic Cult that appeared in Hangzhou this time seems to be only a part of them. Am I right?”

“Yes. That’s likely.”

“Then... if you combine the forces of those who didn’t appear in the Central Plains this time, how strong would the Demonic Cult be?”

Chung Myung frowned slightly. Counting on his fingers and thinking deeply, he finally looked at Hyun Jong with a serious face.

“...You mean their strength excluding the Heavenly Demon?”

“Yes.”

“Then...”

He closed his mouth tightly again. Chung Myung, silent as if pondering something, finally spoke when everyone was tense and dry-mouthed.

“At least ten times, no... even more.”

The air in the room instantly froze cold.

“...Ten times?”

Five Swords’s expression hardened seriously.

They already suspected that the forces they encountered this time were not the main body of the demonic cult. Even without considering others, the mere presence of the Heaven Killer was enough to make a solid guess.

‘But...’

Baek Cheon glanced at Chung Myung, his eyes filled with despair.

Even with only the demonic cult members present, the elites of the Ten Thousand People Clan and the Black Ghost Fort were in danger. But what if Dan Ja-Kang had withdrawn the cultists and, instead of taking the front line, had waited leisurely?

Perhaps everyone who entered Hangzhou would have met their end there.

‘But ten times that force?’

It was truly an absurd statement.

Hyun Jong, perhaps feeling the same as Five Swords, shook his head repeatedly.

“...It’s beyond what I imagined. No... To be honest, it’s hard to believe all this.”

His voice conveyed childish bewilderment and frustration to everyone.

“Of course, the Demonic Cult... was the force that once drove the murim world to the brink of destruction. But... it’s been a full hundred years since then...”

“That’s why.”

Chung Myung replied calmly. When Hyun Jong looked at him as if asking for clarification, he elaborated.

“Taking that into account, it’s about ten times. If it were any less, it wouldn’t stop at just ten times.”

Hyun Jong still looked doubtful. However, in Chung Myung’s eyes, there was no trace of wavering. Seeing that confident expression, Hyun Jong had no choice but to believe him. Whenever that boy spoke with such certainty, there was always a solid basis behind it.

“Could you explain the reason a bit?”

“It’s simple. Because of the high priest.”

“High priest?”

“Yes.”

Chung Myung nodded and continued.

“The high priest we faced was young. That means the Demonic Cult, driven out of the Central Plains, is currently expanding its followers and creating new forces in some way.”

He glanced around at those seated nearby and said,

“And that means... there could be many more like that young high priest in the Demonic Cult now.”

Silence fell.

Im So-Byeong, pressing his cheek with a fan, raised a hand.

“No. Wait a moment, Taoist.”

His brows furrowed.

“Of course, it’s possible. We’ve only seen two high priests so far. There could be many others. But simply estimating it that way is...”

“The Demonic Cult has seventeen sub-cults in total.”

Im So-Byeong sealed his lips tightly.

He had heard this before. In the past, the Heavenly Demon led the Demonic Cult, which consisted of seventeen districts, to invade the Central Plains.

“And each sub-cult is led by a high priest. In other words, a proper Demonic Cult has seventeen high priests.”

“Seventeen...”

At that moment, Baek Cheon felt chills run down his spine.

The image of Dan Ja-Kang, who emitted demonic qi like a storm, flashed through his mind—a terrifying incarnation of fear. The formidable figure who overwhelmed even the mighty Desolate Gold Elder, Jang Ilso, and Chung Myung.

‘That guy... seventeen of them?’

His fingertips trembled.

In the suffocating silence, Chung Myung’s eyes darkened.

Generally, the number of high priests is known to be about ten. But that was because the Demonic Cult could not replenish the high priests who died in the war where they poured all their strength, so only an approximate number was known.

If the Demonic Cult had proper strength, the number of high priests would be seventeen. Moreover, if those who do not lead a sub-cult but serve only the Heavenly Demon in the most honorable position are also called high priests, the number should be even higher.

“Hmm. But Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

Tang Gunak also spoke with a stern face, as if objecting.

“The current Demonic Cult is different from the past. Weren’t they driven out of the Central Plains? It’s hard to say they’ve recovered their strength again...”

“Then what about before?”

“...What do you mean?”

“Did the Demonic Cult grow in the Central Plains before?”

Tang Gunak fell silent. It was such obvious logic, but he had never really considered it until now.

Chung Myung said, as if to show him,

“The previous Demonic Cult also grew its power outside the Central Plains. Yet, centered on the Heavenly Demon, it fully prepared and invaded the Central Plains. Is there any guarantee it would be different this time?”

“...There isn’t.”

When Tang Gunak admitted this, Chung Myung nodded.

“They are now growing their strength to invade the Central Plains again. So above all, they are putting all their effort into raising proper high priests. One high priest exerts more overwhelming power than clumsily raising a thousand followers.”

In the murim world, increasing the number of absolute masters even by one is far more important than simply increasing numbers.

“And they have had a hundred years.”

Chung Myung’s gaze grew colder.

“While the Central Plains forgot the past and indulged in peace, they licked their wounds, waiting for the day to take revenge on the Central Plains. For a full hundred years.”

“...”

“But is it really possible that the only newly raised high priest is just that one? Surely not?”

Tang Gunak sighed deeply.

Raising an absolute warrior is never easy. No matter how much effort a sect puts in, if they can raise even one per generation, it can be considered a success.

But... if given a hundred years?

If those defeated in the Central Plains had poured all their strength into nurturing successors from that moment until now? If the Demonic Cult, which as a single sect drove the entire Central Plains to the brink of destruction, had been sharpening themselves for a hundred years?

‘...I can’t even say it’s impossible.’

Understanding the situation, Tang Gunak looked at Chung Myung with a stern face.

“Then you think... the Demonic Cult has fully recovered its strength?”

“To be honest...”

Chung Myung paused, pondered for a moment, then spoke again.

“In terms of numbers, I think they haven’t reached the past level. The number of high priests is probably about ten at most now. That’s the optimistic view.”

Everyone nodded.

Having ten like Dan Ja-Kang is chilling enough, but compared to seventeen, it’s a more realistic number.

“The problem is the quality of the high priests.”

“Hmm?”

“The high priests who survived when the Heavenly Demon died are still in the Demonic Cult. Do you understand what that means?”

“...The high priests from that time are alive? Then how old are they?”

Unconcealable horror bloomed in Tang Gunak’s eyes.

“That’s not what’s important. What’s important is that they have become stronger than before.”

The body naturally deteriorates with age.

Even martial artists who defy the natural order cannot avoid this. But inner strength only accumulates over time. If the powerful warriors who drove the martial world to the brink of destruction continued training for a hundred years, no one could guess their current level.

“That young high priest was called the Second High Priest.”

“...So there is also an Arch High Priest above him. That means at least two high priests have survived from the past until now?”

“Yes. At the very least.”

Chung Myung bit his lip slightly.

“Just their existence alone means the total strength of the high priests, including those from the past, has become stronger than before.”

Everyone fell silent. Chung Myung understood this silence.

Who would have imagined that someone survived for such a long time?

When Chung Myung died, Tang Jo-pyung was just a child, but now he has become the eldest of the Tang family, with only a little time left before his death.

But to think that the high priests who were not young even then are still alive...

Of course, it’s not entirely impossible. Didn’t Chung Myung see with his own eyes the high priest of the North Sea who survived the past war?

But this case is different. The North Sea high priest was just a rookie inserted clumsily to fill the serious high priest vacancy at the time. Under normal circumstances, neither his age nor his martial skill would allow him to call himself a high priest.

But...

‘Heaven Killer.’

That guy is a completely different case.

He was notoriously infamous even among those terrifying high priests of the past. That guy is still alive. And yet, he has not risen to the position of Arch High Priest.

That fact meant a great deal.

“I declare with certainty.”

The moment those words came out, everyone looked at Chung Myung with tense faces.

“If the Demonic Cult invades the Central Plains again this time, their power will be no less than in the past.”

His tongue felt bitter as he spoke the truth. Even Chung Myung found it hard to say this. No, not long ago, he wouldn’t have dared to say this in front of them.

A moderate enemy fuels ambition, but a despairingly huge enemy even crushes the remaining will.

Chung Myung avoided mentioning the demonic cult as much as possible not only to hide his identity but also because they were not ready to accept the existence of the Demonic Cult.

But now, he could no longer avoid it.

Having seen with his own eyes and felt it on his skin, and being certain that their outbreak is imminent, now they must know the truth.

How huge and strong the enemy they must fight is.

‘If there is hope...’

It was at that very moment.

“There is something I want to confirm.”

Chung Myung’s gaze turned aside. Breaking the heavy atmosphere, it was none other than Baek Cheon who spoke.

Meeting Chung Myung’s gaze directly, he bit his lip slightly and said,

“According to what you said, and as they mentioned... wasn’t more than half of the past Demonic Cult’s strength due to the Heavenly Demon?”

“...That’s probably true.”

Baek Cheon frowned and asked,

“Then your claim that the Demonic Cult is no less than before assumes that the Heavenly Demon returns with the same power as before, right?”

Chung Myung slowly nodded.

“Are you sure?”

“...”

“Does the Heavenly Demon really resurrect with the same power as before? No... resurrection itself is an absurd concept, but to resurrect with full power is even stranger.”

Everyone nodded with sudden realization at Baek Cheon’s question.

Perhaps the most important topic in this discussion is the Heavenly Demon. Yet ironically, they knew so little about it that they overlooked it.

But Chung Myung’s answer was a bit different from their expectations.

“I don’t know.”

“...What?”

“I don’t know either.”

Chung Myung said firmly with a cold face.

“I don’t know how he resurrects. So I can’t predict it for certain. Whether he needs time after resurrection to regain his power, or if he becomes a true ‘Heavenly Demon’ the moment he resurrects.”

“...”

“There is only one certainty. No matter how he resurrects... if he is given time, the result will be the same.”

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint Chung Myung—at the same time, Plum Blossom Sword Saint Chung Myung.

From his mouth came words like an unavoidable fate, having lived two lives with two identities.

“If we cannot stop the Heavenly Demon and the Demonic Cult, what we saw in Hangzhou will be reproduced throughout the entire Central Plains. No, perhaps beyond the Central Plains to the whole world...”

Perhaps a prophecy. At the same time, a curse.

“Destruction will come.”

An unavoidable reality flowed from Chung Myung’s mouth.

Silence lingered for a long time.

The words themselves were not shocking. Half in jest, half as a passing remark, it was a story they had heard many times before. Yet, the same words carry vastly different weight depending on when and where they are spoken.

The heavy weight of the word “destruction” seemed to crush their very bodies.

Especially for those who had witnessed the devastation of Hangzhou with their own eyes, these words struck even harder.

“Destruction...”

A solemn voice escaped Baek Cheon’s lips.

They had seen it. Hangzhou, once hailed as the most splendid and beautiful city in the Central Plains, had become a pitiful ruin.

‘Such devastation spreading throughout the entire Central Plains...’

Baek Cheon gritted his teeth tightly. This had to be stopped at all costs.

“This is serious.”

The one who broke the heavy silence was none other than Im So-Byeong. Scratching his head with the tip of his fan, his face clearly showed signs of distress.

“It’s far too serious to simply brush off.”

The usual smile that habitually graced his face had long since vanished.

“If the Evil Sect equips itself with full strength as the temple predicts and invades the Central Plains, doesn’t that mean that in our current state, the Central Plains cannot stop them?”

Im So-Byeong’s gaze was sharper and colder than usual. Chung Myung quietly nodded.

“That’s probably the case.”

“...This is a headache.”

Im So-Byeong sighed deeply.

When an external enemy invades, internal conflicts are usually resolved. No, they must be resolved. But he knew it was not as simple as it sounded.

Historically, there have been cases where internal strife was settled due to foreign invasions, but countless times, internal conflicts persisted, leading to the country’s destruction both inside and out.

Im So-Byeong sighed again and shrugged his shoulders.

“Of course, it depends on when the Demonic Cult rises, but the situation is indeed grim.”

Naturally, the faces of the abbot and Jang Ilso came to everyone’s mind.

The world is now divided into three forces.

The Nine Great Sects centered around Shaolin’s abbot, the Four Sect Alliance led by Jang Ilso, and the Heavenly Friends Alliance centered around Mount Hua.

Each faction wields immense influence in their respective strongholds.

Im So-Byeong scratched his cheek, feeling uneasy.

‘Rather...’

Yes, perhaps if the Demonic Cult had risen five years ago, the situation might have been better. Five years ago, neither the Heavenly Friends Alliance nor the Four Sect Alliance existed.

If the Demonic Cult had risen then, whether it was Mount Hua or Jang Ilso, they would have had no choice but to unite around Shaolin. Survival comes first.

But now, the situation is different.

Over the past five years, Shaolin has lost so much. Comparing its status five years ago to now is embarrassing. Back then, Shaolin was regarded as the North Star of the martial world, protecting the realm, but now it has lost all its prestige and struggles even to control the internal conflicts within the Nine Great Sects.

In contrast, what about the Ten Thousand People Clan and Mount Hua? They have built tremendous prestige, incomparable to five years ago.

‘Along with that, the mutual animosity as well...’

What determines a person’s attitude are disposition and emotions. If the abbot, Chung Myung, and Jang Ilso had met five years ago, they might have found each other peculiar but would not have thought to threaten each other’s lives.

But now, it is hard to imagine these three sitting in the same space. The chasm of animosity they have built is wide and deep.

‘An alliance of such three forces...’

From Im So-Byeong’s perspective, the revival of the Heavenly Demon or the Demonic Cult’s invasion seemed less absurd than this.

His gaze reached Chung Myung.

‘What is he thinking right now?’

The Chung Myung he knew always looked farther than anyone else. Even Im So-Byeong, who prides himself on controlling most things, only realized Chung Myung’s arrangements after some time and was startled more than once.

“Chung Myung.”

Hyun Jong, sharing Im So-Byeong’s thoughts, quietly called Chung Myung.

“Yes, Sect Leader.”

“I want to ask you something. If the situation is truly that serious, what do you think we should do from now on?”

Hyun Jong sighed deeply.

“If we didn’t know, ignorance would be bliss, but now that we know, we cannot remain idle. We must reduce the damage as much as possible.”

“That’s right, Sect Leader.”

Others nodded in agreement with Hyun Jong’s words, but Chung Myung remained silent and unmoving.

“According to you, it seems that only if the entire Central Plains unite is there a chance. Is that correct?”

“...That is likely.”

Hyun Jong nodded with his eyes closed. Then, opening them, he looked straight at Chung Myung with eyes full of determination.

“Then I ask you...”

“...”

“What do you think is the probability that the Nine Great Sects, the Four Sect Alliance, and we can form an alliance?”

The disciples around immediately reacted to the question.

“Impossible, Sect Leader.”

“We cannot join hands with Jang Ilso!”

“That man is absolutely unacceptable.”

Surprised by the fierce opposition, Hyun Jong looked around. Then, Un Geom spoke on behalf of everyone.

“Sect Leader, I understand what you are thinking. I too respect your cause to unite even with those Evil Sect bastards for the greater good of all under heaven. However...”

Un Geom spoke with resolute eyes.

“Jang Ilso is not someone who can be negotiated with. We have clearly seen what he has done in Hangzhou.”

“...Hmm.”

If someone other than Un Geom had said this, it might have been dismissed as youthful rashness ignoring the greater cause.

But Un Geom is not such a person. He is arguably the calmest person in Mount Hua. For him to say this means there is something felt only by those who have directly experienced Jang Ilso.

At that moment, Tang Gunak also spoke.

“And Alliance Lord, negotiating with the Nine Great Sects is also difficult.”

“...Lord Tang.”

Tang Gunak shook his head with a heavy expression.

“What they value most is pride and the interests of their sects. Would they accept the alliance proposed by the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

Hyun Jong could not deny this and remained silent.

“Even if an alliance is formed, it will not be as solid as you think, Alliance Lord. Like the past Five Lords of Shenzhou, it will be a case of different people dreaming different dreams.”

Hearing this, a sigh escaped naturally. This was likely true.

Then Chung Myung spoke.

“No. It won’t be as difficult as you think.”

“Chung Myung!”

“Man! What are you saying!”

The startled disciples of Mount Hua looked at Chung Myung in shock. But Chung Myung simply stared at Hyun Jong with an indifferent expression.

“Forming an alliance with the Nine Great Sects and bringing in Jang Ilso might actually be easier than expected.”

“No, that’s not as easy as you say...”

“If they want something, give them what they want.”

Chung Myung firmly cut off Im So-Byeong’s words. Im So-Byeong closed his mouth like a clam.

Chung Myung glanced over everyone present and continued.

“If they want justification, give them justification.”

“...”

“If they want benefits, give them benefits.”

Hyun Jong’s gaze darkened momentarily.

“If Shaolin wants the honor of leading the murim world, they can bow before them in the name of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. Just the justification of having secured the surrender of the Heavenly Friends Alliance would allow the abbot to cover up all his past misdeeds, so he would comply with any request.”

“That is...”

Tang Gunak opened his mouth as if to say something but then stopped.

In fact, this was a very realistic proposal. What the abbot desires most now is to restore Shaolin’s lost honor. If the prestigious Heavenly Friends Alliance and Mount Hua’s Sword Saint bow before the abbot, all past failures would seem like minor flaws.

The abbot would even join hands with his mortal enemies for such benefits.

“If Jang Ilso wants practical benefits, give him practical benefits. What he wants is to advance into the northern river region and expand the Four Sect Alliance’s territory. If the Heavenly Friends Alliance cedes the Yangtze River basin to Jang Ilso and appropriately grants territory extending to Sichuan and Shaanxi, Jang Ilso will reluctantly accept the proposal.”

“...Ugh.”

This time, Im So-Byeong groaned.

That would certainly be an unsatisfactory outcome for Jang Ilso, but it is enough bait to bring him to the negotiating table.

There is something to gain without shedding a drop of blood, so that damn guy won’t back out. Especially considering the huge price to be paid to control the Yangtze basin and the western lands of the Central Plains.

“So... if you want, an alliance is possible.”

Hyun Jong did not speak hastily but quietly looked at Chung Myung. He now knew well that when this boy said such things, he hid his true intentions behind them.

“One thing, Sect Leader.”

As expected, Chung Myung spoke in a serious tone.

“That means we must sacrifice ourselves for the greater good.”

“That much...”

“Of course, at first, we can give up something small. But once the alliance is formed that way, to maintain it, more and more must be given to others. If no one wants to sacrifice, in the end, I will have to sacrifice.”

“...”

“At first, a finger.”

A chilling coldness began to seep into Chung Myung’s voice.

“Then an arm.”

“...”

“Eventually, all limbs will be given, and finally, even the neck must be laid down.”

Chung Myung’s gaze swept over everyone coldly.

“For the greater good of peace in the Central Plains.”

Hyun Jong’s face hardened. He understood better than anyone what Chung Myung meant by these words.

“If that is done, perhaps peace in the Central Plains can be maintained. But Sect Leader... there is one thing you must know.”

Chung Myung’s voice was like a blade throughout. It contained advice, concern, and a deep regret that others could not feel.

“In the peaceful Central Plains preserved that way, Mount Hua will have no place. Sect Leader, you may watch from the afterlife as Mount Hua becomes a forgotten relic... or worse, a subject of scorn and ridicule, unable even to be fully destroyed.”

Hyun Jong’s eyes trembled slightly.

“Yet if that is the cause you wish to protect, Sect Leader.”

Chung Myung paused for a moment. Then he looked at each disciple around him as if engraving their faces in his memory.

“If that is the path Mount Hua wishes to take...”

“...”

“I will simply follow.”

Chung Myung quietly closed his eyes.

In the darkened world, one person was shedding tears of blood, looking at him.

‘Chung Mun sahyung...’

Chung Myung’s fist clenched tightly on his thigh.

It’s not that Chung Myung didn’t understand the fear they felt. In fact, he understood their feelings more deeply than anyone else—because he had already experienced it once before.

When the Demonic Cult began pushing into the Central Plains, the moment he grasped their overwhelming strength and purpose intellectually, Mount Hua was gripped by suffocating fear and pressure. The fear that if nothing was done, the Central Plains might be destroyed. And the pressure that Mount Hua must share that responsibility. Burdened by that weight, Mount Hua ultimately rushed forward to sacrifice itself once before.

That was surely the right choice. Because as a result, the Central Plains defeated the Heavenly Demon and stopped the demonic cult. But...

“Chung Mun sahyung.”

Chung Myung addressed Chung Mun, who was shedding tears of blood.

“Do you still believe that choice was right, Sahyung?”

There was no need to hear an answer. The tears Chung Mun shed spoke for themselves.

If there really is a Heavenly realm, and Chung Mun watched with his own eyes the disgrace Mount Hua and their descendants suffered in their downfall, then for Chung Mun, that place would be more fittingly called an endless hell rather than a Heavenly realm. How could a place that forces one to witness scenes more agonizing than burning in hell be called a Heavenly realm?

And now, Hyun Jong is walking that path.

Chung Myung opened his eyes and looked at Hyun Jong. The face of Chung Mun overlapped with Hyun Jong’s face sitting before him.

“Sect leader.”

“...Speak.”

“I know it might be ridiculous for me to say this.”

“...You do know that, huh, you brat.”

That one phrase from Chung Myung slightly eased the heavy atmosphere.

Chung Myung was always like that. Even when everyone tried to stop him, he would rush into enemy lines without hesitation because it was his duty. So it didn’t make sense that now he was trying to stop Hyun Jong.

“Disciple, I have thought long and hard. Why did Mount Hua fall despite upholding the greater cause and doing what was right?”

“...”

“Why did it have to shed tears of blood even though it did the right thing?”

Hyun Jong closed his eyes slightly. In truth, that question was an unsolvable riddle for those living in Mount Hua now.

They were taught to uphold their concept of righteousness. But the price of keeping those agreements was devastating. The gap between what was learned and what was experienced sometimes made them doubt their path.

If it were about protecting others with their own death, everyone here would do it without hesitation.

But Mount Hua’s disciples knew that what they were about to do would push even those they most wanted to protect into the yoke of sacrifice.

How can one accept that the price of trying to protect loved ones is the sacrifice of those very people?

Can they really die smiling?

Can someone who witnessed with their own eyes the horrific scene of comrades who trained, laughed, and joked together all dying truly feel joy for preventing the Central Plains’ destruction and die smiling?

Now, Chung Myung had brought up the fact that Mount Hua’s disciples had implicitly tried not to mention.

“...Have you thought about it?”

“Yes.”

“So... have you found the answer?”

Chung Myung slowly shook his head.

“I have not found it, sect leader.”

“...”

“I only hoped. That we would become stronger. And that they would be weaker than before. So that even if the same thing repeated, the outcome wouldn’t be as tragic as before.”

Hyun Jong closed his eyes.

It was an empty and futile answer. But Hyun Jong was no different from Chung Myung. To hold onto the facade of agreement and righteousness, they merely hoped—hoping that nothing would ever threaten their lives.

“Therefore, when I heard the Demonic Cult had appeared, I had to go. Not to stop them, but to confirm. To see with my own eyes how strong they were.”

Hyun Jong’s eyelids trembled.

Because he could anticipate the despair Chung Myung must have felt upon seeing the Demonic Cult’s power with his own eyes. No, he could clearly feel it in that gaze.

“...You must have felt so desperate.”

“Yes.”

Chung Myung answered calmly. So calm that it was painful. A sigh escaped from Hyun Jong’s lips.

Why did this boy always keep such stories inside himself?

If only he would share a little more of what he holds inside, he would somehow help. No... even if he couldn’t help, he would at least try his best.

“I fought without finding the answer. Because fighting is better than not fighting.”

“...”

“But through fighting, I came to understand why Mount Hua failed.”

Hyun Jong looked at Chung Myung with a dazed expression.

“Why?”

“Because it did not believe.”

“...Did not believe?”

“Yes.”

Chung Myung slowly nodded.

Chung Mun was undoubtedly a great person. Not in martial strength, but in human strength, there was no one comparable even in Chung Myung’s lifetime. Even Hyun Jong, whom Chung Myung acknowledges now, might seem weak compared to Chung Mun.

But...

“Back then, Mount Hua was very strong, and its leader was excellent. Therefore... precisely because of that, Mount Hua could trust no one but itself.”

Only now did he understand why Mount Hua had to bear that heavy burden alone.

Chung Mun always told him not to go alone but to care for his fellow disciples. Not to go alone, but to lead the less capable with his excellence. That was Chung Mun’s way. But...

Chung Myung bit his lip slightly.

Perhaps this was the first time in his long life that he said this...

“That way was wrong.”

The moment he spoke, his tongue burned. Denying Chung Mun was like denying all of Chung Myung. But... now Chung Myung had to do this bitter thing.

Not for those left in the past, but for those living now.

“The reason Mount Hua could fight at the forefront was because there were those supporting from behind.”

Chung Myung could fight at the front because there were people supporting him.

“Yet Mount Hua believed it did everything alone.”

Still, Chung Myung believed he did everything himself.

“If Mount Hua had even a little time to look back at those fighting behind...”

If Chung Myung could have looked at those fighting behind even a little.

“The outcome might have been different.”

They might not have lost them all.

Having said that much, Chung Myung quietly closed his eyes.

Mount Hua of that era was certainly a strong and great sect. But because of that, it was also arrogant and dogmatic in its way. It looked coldly at those who couldn’t understand Mount Hua’s righteousness. This is an undeniable fact.

Chung Mun always scolded Chung Myung for not harmonizing with others. But Mount Hua, with Chung Mun as its leader, might not have been much different from Chung Myung. The harmony Chung Mun spoke of was merely harmony within the sect.

Still, Chung Myung believed Chung Mun was absolutely right.

Because Chung Mun was always the right person and too great for Chung Myung to follow.

But fighting Dan Ja-Kang, he came to realize.

Behind him was Tang Bo, but behind Mount Hua, there was no one.

Even Chung Mun, whom Chung Myung revered like Mount Tai, was not perfect. He was just a human who struggled throughout his life.

And now, for the first time, Chung Myung looks at the world freed from the shadow of Chung Mun that had hung over him—a shadow that was warm but also heavy and deep.

“Sect leader.”

“Yes... yes, Chung Myung.”

“When a person’s heart becomes anxious, they forget what they possess.”

“...”

“What do we have?”

Hyun Jong hesitated to answer. What do we have... what do we possess...

Righteousness? Harmony? Or... force?

Hyun Jong looked around at those seated here with deeply sunken eyes. Then, as if having no more thoughts, he spoke.

“Mount Hua has many things. But if I had to name the most precious and important one, there is only one.”

Hyun Jong nodded with a slight smile.

“It’s just people.”

Chung Myung smiled back at those words. It was exactly what he hoped Hyun Jong would say.

“It’s different from the past. We have people. Not just Mount Hua, but those who stand with Mount Hua.”

Hyun Jong nodded slightly.

Since Chung Myung came, Mount Hua gained many things. But the greatest among them is undoubtedly relationships and bonds.

“I was the same. When crushed by the power of the Four Sects Alliance, the first thing I chose was simply to increase my own strength. I was willing to give up everything for that.”

“Yes, that’s right.”

“Originally, the Heavenly Friends Alliance should have fallen then. Who would wait for someone who abandoned all responsibility to grow their own power? But... the leader of the Tang family bore even the tasks we had to do and silently waited for us.”

Chung Myung turned his head to look at Tang Gunak. Receiving that gaze, Tang Gunak gave an awkward smile as if embarrassed at the praise.

“Why did you do that, family lord?”

“Is there any particular reason? Just...”

It was an embarrassed voice rarely heard from Tang Gunak. So instead, it felt full of genuine sincerity without a hint of doubt.

“Because we are close friends.”

As if embarrassed to say such words himself, Tang Gunak lightly cleared his throat. Chung Myung smiled warmly.

If at that time,

When they lost what they should never have lost in the past.

If there had been a sect like the Tang family in Mount Hua, if there had been someone like Tang Bo for Chung Myung in Mount Hua, would they have just watched Mount Hua’s downfall? Would they have stood by as a pack of wolves rushed toward Mount Hua?

No, absolutely not. There’s no way.

Tang Bo would have risked his life to stop it. The current Tang family would never have let them set foot on Mount Hua’s gates no matter what happened.

What Mount Hua has now but did not have then.

“Sect leader...”

This is a word from Chung Myung, a disciple of Mount Hua, to Sect leader Hyun Jong, and also from the Plum Blossom Sword Saint Chung Myung to the past Chung Mun.

“A swordsman wavering before a powerful enemy must trust his sword and the time he has spent training.”

“...That’s right.”

“Then what should a sect believe in?”

“Well...”

Hyun Jong looked once more at those seated before him. Then, with gentle eyes, he answered Chung Myung.

“The value of what it has protected itself... and the conviction in the path it has walked.”

“Yes, sect leader.”

Chung Myung also smiled softly.

“The reason we formed the Heavenly Friends Alliance was because we needed people to trust and fight alongside each other. Because we believed we would be strength and fortress for one another.”

“Yes, that’s right.”

Chung Myung slowly bowed his head.

“So, sect leader, when you are troubled about what to do, look back at what Mount Hua has protected. I believe all answers lie within.”

Hyun Jong quietly gazed at the bowed Chung Myung.

What they have done. What they have protected.

“...I understand what you mean.”

Chung Myung closed his eyes.

‘Sect leader sahyung.’

In the darkness, Chung Mun was still watching him. But Chung Mun’s face was no longer filled with pain.

‘Don’t worry. The children now are better than us.’

At that moment, a gentle smile blossomed on Chung Mun’s lips as he looked at him.

Chung Myung closed his eyes for a long time, gazing at that smile.

For a long time.

At the edge of the large pavilion’s eaves, Chung Myung stood holding a bottle of liquor in one hand, staring blankly at the moon.

A little farther away, Five Swords watched him. Normally, Tang Soso would have gone wild if a patient drank alcohol, but even he couldn’t bring himself to rush over now and simply observed quietly.

“What do you think he’s thinking about?”

“I don’t know.”

Baek Cheon shook his head in response to Tang Soso’s question.

“He’s someone whose mind you just can’t read.”

“...That’s true.”

Yoon Jong and Jo Gul sighed simultaneously. The expressions of the Mount Hua disciples watching Chung Myung were quite complicated.

“Sahyung.”

“Why?”

“How does Chung Myung know all those things?”

When Yoon Jong didn’t answer, Jo Gul glanced at him cautiously and continued.

“Well... isn’t it a bit strange? Of course, if Chung Myung comes from a place that held a grudge against the Demonic Cult in the past, it wouldn’t be odd for him to know some details about them...”

Jo Gul stopped mid-sentence and quietly closed his mouth. What he originally wanted to say was, ‘Doesn’t he know too much even for that?’

But he couldn’t bring himself to say it. Discussing Chung Myung’s past was something they tacitly avoided.

No one explicitly forbade talking about it, but everyone naturally steered clear because they knew it would make everyone uncomfortable.

After this incident, what confused Jo Gul the most was Chung Myung’s reaction when he saw the Demonic Cult’s high priest.

‘He recognized him.’

There were countless possible scenarios.

For example, Chung Myung’s ancestor might have been a Mount Hua person who fled before the battle with the Demonic Cult at the Hundred Thousand Mountains because he couldn’t bear the burden.

So even though he knew all the martial arts of Mount Hua, he couldn’t return because of guilt—like Yu Yiseol’s father.

A great sect falling into ruin. How many people wouldn’t have stories tied to it?

That’s why no one had tried to dig into Chung Myung’s past until now.

But now Jo Gul began to think differently.

‘Even if that were the case... could he recognize the high priest from a hundred years ago? Would someone passing down history really describe the high priests’ appearances in such detail?’

It was unknowable. But one thing was certain: Chung Myung’s knowledge of the Demonic Cult went beyond what could be explained by hearsay.

“If it’s strange?”

“...Yes?”

At that moment, Yoon Jong turned to Jo Gul with dark eyes.

“Why? Are you trying to suspect Chung Myung now?”

“Ah, no, that’s...”

Yoon Jong frowned and said firmly.

“Don’t say useless things. No matter how he knows it, Chung Myung has done his best to help us all this time. That fact will never change.”

“I know that too...”

Jo Gul lowered his head with a sullen face. He couldn’t deny Yoon Jong’s words, nor did he want to.

“Come to think of it, it feels like just yesterday I got hit by that guy and slammed into the ceiling.”

Looking back, time really flew by.

Who would have imagined Mount Hua would come to this?

“...Come to think of it, everything happened after that guy came into Mount Hua.”

“Huh?”

“Right? If it weren’t for him, would we even be worrying about the future of the murim world?”

Yoon Jong, reflecting quietly, looked at Chung Myung with new eyes.

If it weren’t for Chung Myung, Jo Gul would have returned to his family. Yoon Jong would have guarded the deserted Mount Hua alone. Yu Yiseol might have stayed somehow, but Tang Soso wouldn’t have come, and Baek Cheon’s fate would be uncertain.

No, even before that, Mount Hua might have collapsed, scattering everyone to find their own paths.

“It all started when that guy suddenly joined Mount Hua.”

“...That’s right.”

Yoon Jong answered softly and fell into thought for a moment.

What was Chung Myung thinking then?

Looking back now, it’s clear Chung Myung wanted to make Mount Hua great again. How could he think that walking into such a ruined sect?

“...I just don’t know.”

Just as Jo Gul bitterly smacked his lips, Yoon Jong glanced back with a very reluctant look.

“Um... sasuk.”

“Yes?”

“...It’s a bit strange that I told Jo Gul to shut up and now I’m saying this, but I’ve had a somewhat plausible... no, rather a somewhat... anyway, I’ve had a thought.”

Unlike Yoon Jong, his preamble was long and his tone odd.

“What on earth is it?”

“Well... about that Chung Myung guy.”

Baek Cheon’s eyes hardened slightly. He felt uncomfortable bringing up a topic already discussed once.

“The reason we’re looking at Chung Myung with some suspicion... no, ambiguous eyes is because he knows too much about the past Demonic Cult, right?”

“That’s right... isn’t it?”

“He also knows too much about the war a hundred years ago. So, um... maybe...”

When Yoon Jong paused slightly, everyone swallowed dryly.

“Uh, maybe what?”

Hearing the urging voices, Yoon Jong nervously glanced at Chung Myung and spoke.

“Well... that Chung Myung guy...”

Then he squeezed his eyes shut and blurted out.

“Could he be from the Demonic Cult?”

A moment of silence followed.

Baek Cheon’s eyes widened. Those standing nearby also stared wide-eyed and open-mouthed.

Baek Cheon stammered with a flustered face.

“Ah, no... no... you bastard. That’s nonsense...”

But at that moment, someone clapped loudly beside him.

“That’s... convincing!”

“...The mystery is all solved.”

“Huh?”

Baek Cheon looked around dumbfounded. The sahyung-sajae all nodded with faces full of great realization.

“Come to think of it, he fits the Demonic Cult more than the Justice faction.”

“...Then it makes sense why he knows so much about the Heavenly Demon sect.”

“His personality too.”

“Right, his attitude! His personality!”

“Ah, no...”

Cold sweat began to trickle down Baek Cheon’s neat forehead.

Such absurd, ridiculous, and very plausible... no! Damn it! Plausible! What am I thinking!

“Say something reasonable! Who hates the Demonic Cult more than Chung Myung here? His hatred is at a level that needs medical treatment and counseling.”

“Look, sasuk.”

At that moment, Jo Gul clicked his tongue and said.

“Sasuk probably grew up too sheltered to know.”

“It sounds a bit sharp coming from you.”

“...Let’s move past that.”

Jo Gul arrogantly gestured toward Baek Cheon and asked.

“Which sect does sasuk hate the most?”

“Southern Edge.”

Without hesitation, the answer came immediately. Jo Gul nodded as if to say, ‘See?’

“Where is sasuk originally from?”

“...Southern Edge.”

“Anything else to say?”

Wow... this can’t be refuted.

“People who turn their backs are the scariest. If that guy was once a Demonic Cult member who got pissed off and ran away, it all makes sense. His terrible personality and ridiculous martial arts.”

Listening to Jo Gul, Yoon Jong added seriously.

“If it were someone else, maybe not, but that guy might think, ‘I hate the Demonic Cult, so I’ll go to Mount Hua, which the sect hates most, and shake it up.’ That’s the kind of guy he is.”

Baek Cheon quietly closed his mouth.

Even Baek Cheon once got so pissed at Jin Geum-Ryong and Southern Edge that he charged into Mount Hua, their most hated sect. Whether this theory is possible or not, Baek Cheon has no right to say it’s impossible.

If he said ‘That can’t be’ here, he’d surely get bombarded with ‘Hey? So who was the one who did what even a Demonic Cult member wouldn’t do?’

“...No, enough nonsense.”

“I’m saying there’s a possibility.”

“Not just a possibility, it’s high.”

“Confirmed.”

Baek Cheon pressed his temples hard. He couldn’t tell if these guys were serious or joking anymore. His head was throbbing.

He sighed deeply.

“Anyway... if there’s something to say, he’ll say it. You know that Chung Myung doesn’t tell us unnecessary things, but always tells us what’s necessary.”

“That’s true.”

“He knows about the Demonic Cult but hasn’t mentioned it to us until now. You know why, right?”

“...Yes.”

Everyone nodded. It was impossible not to know. If they had learned the true power of the Demonic Cult three years ago, they might have lost heart.

Even now, they were dumbfounded by the gap with the Demonic Cult; back then, it must have been worse.

Chung Myung still held the bottle, staring blankly at the moon. Baek Cheon glanced at him and spoke calmly.

“In other words, he thinks we’ve grown enough to handle knowing all those truths now.”

“...”

“So let’s wait a little longer. When the time comes for us to know, he will surely tell us.”

“Yes, sasuk.”

Yoon Jong nodded. Then he glanced at everyone and lowered his voice a bit.

“More than that... well... I’m not sure if I’m the only one thinking this, but...”

“What thought?”

“Did sasuk understand the conversation between the sect leader and Chung Myung earlier?”

Baek Cheon didn’t answer. Yoon Jong awkwardly scratched his head.

“I felt it ended ambiguously... so I don’t really know the conclusion. If you understood, could you tell me?”

“Uh... well...”

Baek Cheon’s face was very reluctant.

Because he also didn’t fully understand the last conversation between the two. What exactly did ‘just do as the Heavenly Friends Alliance has been doing’ mean?

“Honestly, I don’t really...”

“I’ll explain that!”

“Ah, damn! You scared me!”

Startled by the sudden voice, Baek Cheon stepped back.

Im So-Byeong had appeared, gently waving a fan.

“Hehehehehe.”

“...”

“Hehehehehe.”

Im So-Byeong fully opened the fan, half covering his mouth, and laughed unpleasantly.

“...Why are you like this? There must be a reason.”

“Ah, sorry. I just felt amused thinking about it.”

Im So-Byeong shrugged and got to the point.

“It’s nothing special. Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, that is, Taoist Chung Myung, has declared they will unify the murim world under the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“Ah, so that’s it... What, you crazy bastard?”

Baek Cheon’s pupils trembled as if an earthquake struck.

“What? The Kangho Murim world?”

“What are you talking about?”

As eyes filled with blame and scorn turned toward him, Im So-Byeong clicked his tongue.

“Tsk tsk tsk. Such a lack of awareness.”

“...I think it’s not that we lack awareness, but that our minds are absent.”

Namgung Do-Wei, watching this scene from a short distance, was sweating so much that his back was soaked.

‘Is it really okay for him to be like that?’

Still, he is the Green Forest King who rules all the mountains of the Central Plains.

Of course, the Green Forest King is somewhat younger, frail, and a bit... well, a little immature than expected, but still, the Green Forest King is the Green Forest King. Normally, a mere disciple of Mount Hua wouldn’t be allowed to speak so bluntly...

“Tsk!”

As expected, Im So-Byeong showed a strong sign of discomfort and clicked his tongue loudly. Namgung Do-Wei tensed and shrugged his shoulders at that reaction.

“This is why you shouldn’t talk with uneducated folks! What do these sects know? They don’t even know what the Four Books and Three Classics are. Just reading Taoist scriptures, how could they possibly grasp the noble meaning of Confucian scholars?”

“Noble Confucian scholars?”

“This is why Confucius said, ‘A gentleman does not speak of strange powers, disorder, or spirits.’ One should not converse with peculiar Taoists.”

Baek Cheon looked at Im So-Byeong with a dumbfounded expression.

“Is that something a bandit should be saying?”

“Heh heh. Confucius said that if one observes the Nine Thoughts and Nine Behaviors, one is a gentleman. So what does mere origin matter? If you want to refute me, at least master the Three Classics first.”

Hearing that, Baek Cheon snorted.

“It seems the Green Forest King doesn’t know well.”

“Yes?”

“The Book of Changes (I Ching), one of the Three Classics, is a must-read for Taoism.”

“...Really?”

“Tsk tsk. This is why the Evil Sect is like that. How could a wild sect member raised in the mountains possibly grasp the noble meaning of Taoism?”

Eventually, Namgung Do-Wei’s head drooped. He gave up trying to interpret this insane conversation.

“Anyway!”

Im So-Byeong, as if never discouraged, brightened the mood and spread his fan wide.

“The conversation between the Alliance Leader and Taoist Chung Myung is simple. It means that in facing the Demonic Cult in the future, they will not break the foundation of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“So what does that mean?”

“What is the foundation of the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

“Well...”

Baek Cheon glanced at his comrades. They all awkwardly cleared their throats and turned their heads. Baek Cheon grimaced. The answer was his responsibility.

“It’s hard to say I know for sure... but isn’t it about being with trusted friends?”

“Exactly.”

“...Oh, really?”

“...”

Im So-Byeong gave a contemptuous look, and Baek Cheon quietly turned his head away.

“...It’s accurate, but one must add something. The most distinctive feature of the Heavenly Friends Alliance is that it does not discriminate by status or origin.”

Baek Cheon nodded in agreement.

That is the biggest reason why the Heavenly Friends Alliance is now considered a sect among the Justice faction.

“From the start, the Heavenly Friends Alliance was like that. Though it is called a prestigious sect in the murim world, isn’t it true that some secretly despise the Sichuan Tang family?”

“Who the hell says that?”

“...Soso, calm down, don’t curse him.”

“It pisses me off.”

“Whoa whoa. Now, be good.”

Baek Cheon gave a wry smile. Though Tang Soso ground her teeth, this was an unavoidable fact.

The Sichuan Tang family is definitely a prestigious and powerful one. No one can deny their status and skill.

However, because they use poison and assassination, the murim world subtly regards them as cowardly and tends to see them closer to the Evil Sect, despising them.

They just can’t say that openly in front of the Sichuan Tang family members.

“It’s not just the murim world. Since old times, Sichuan Tang has been a despised land even in the Central Plains.”

“...Well, that’s...”

Im So-Byeong shrugged his shoulders.

“What about the Beast Palace and the Northern Sea? They are called the Five Outer Palaces, but the distance between them is twice that between the Central Plains and them. They can’t be grouped as one. The intention behind grouping them is clear.”

“...”

“Those who are not from the Central Plains.”

Baek Cheon silently nodded. This is also true. The term ‘Five Palaces of Outer Wall’ contains contempt for those not from the Central Plains.

“So the beginning of the Heavenly Friends Alliance was a mix centered on Mount Hua, a sect abandoned by Central Plains, gathering despised and neglected sects... Ah, no. Yu Yiseol’s sect. Please put down that sword. I’m not doing this out of ill will...”

Yu Yiseol glared at Im So-Byeong with her sword half drawn, as if daring him to continue.

“Didn’t even trashy Evil Sect guys like the Green Forest get involved?”

Only then did Yu Yiseol sheath her sword and nod.

Im So-Byeong felt somewhat wronged at those words, but what could he do? That sword ghost is scary.

“So what is the identity of the Heavenly Friends Alliance? Justice faction, heretics, foreign powers, Evil Sect, Taoism, secular families... Oh my, there are even apostates here. Should we count them as Buddhists?”

“Who are you calling apostates, who!”

“They gathered all kinds of things in the world, identity or whatever, and just said ‘We’re all friends’ and stirred it all up. Do you know what that’s called?”

“A mess.”

“Destruction.”

“A mixed stew.”

“No, no.”

Im So-Byeong grinned.

“The world calls that the realm.”

“The realm...”

Baek Cheon murmured. Im So-Byeong tapped his neck with his fan. A smile still lingered on his lips.

“The Heavenly Friends Alliance embraces everything. As long as the two words ‘trust’ are fulfilled, it doesn’t discriminate by origin or status. That means anyone can belong to the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“...That’s right. They even accepted bandits.”

“Exactly.”

“That’s what I mean.”

Im So-Byeong looked hurt at Baek Cheon, but Baek Cheon showed a miraculous expression that said, ‘What? Did I say something wrong, bandit?’ and rubbed salt in the Green Forest King’s wound.

“...Therefore, the Heavenly Friends Alliance doesn’t need to divide its forces. If necessary, it can embrace everything within.”

“Well, it’s easier said than done...”

“And the amazing thing about the Heavenly Friends Alliance is not just that. Though it mixes the most diverse people, its cohesion is actually the strongest among the three forces, isn’t it?”

Baek Cheon nodded.

The Nine Great Sects, on the verge of fragmentation, are not even worth discussing.

If you interpret cohesion broadly and include those suppressed by Jang Ilso’s presence, you might rate the Evil Sect Alliance higher, but by common sense, the strongest cohesion among the three forces is undoubtedly the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

“This place is insane. Think about it. No matter how much the Demonic Cult invades, disciples of Mount Hua and Shaolin—two sects with the worst relations in the realm—are joining forces, and Namgung, the leader of the Five Great Families, adds his strength. Even I, the Green Forest King, was dragged here.”

“...”

“The heads of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, Nine Great Sects, Five Great Families, and the Green Forest King. Do you think this is a combination that can naturally mix?”

“...No, I don’t think so.”

Baek Cheon scratched the back of his head. It had seemed natural until now, but in fact, this was an unreasonable situation.

If all those people could get along so easily, why would martial warriors distinguish between Justice faction and Evil Sect, prestigious and minor sects, and thoroughly reject each other?

Even within the same Justice faction, the Nine Great Sects and Five Great Families are divided and subtly check each other.

“But in the Heavenly Friends Alliance, that is possible. So the Heavenly Friends Alliance doesn’t need to conform to others’ thoughts. It just needs to accept them inside! What does that mean?”

“Well, what does it mean?”

“It means there’s no need to become a member of the Alliance. If it just expands its power, the Heavenly Friends Alliance itself will become that Alliance. Whether it’s the Nine Great Sects or the Four Sects Alliance, it can just swallow them up. Not a forced patchwork union, but pushing them under the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“...”

“Heh heh heh heh. I thought he was a big shot, but I never imagined he had such great ambition. Can’t beat him, indeed. Heh heh heh heh.”

Im So-Byeong’s eyes turned to Chung Myung on the eaves, clearly excited.

But Baek Cheon felt something was off about his words.

And at that moment, Yu Yiseol, who had been silent, spoke.

“That it’s different from before.”

“...Hmm?”

Baek Cheon turned to Yu Yiseol. She was looking not at Baek Cheon but at Chung Myung.

“Mount Hua failed. Completely.”

“...”

“Before, we thought it was because of ungrateful people. But that’s not it. Mount Hua failed because it was alone.”

Baek Cheon nodded. This aligns with what Chung Myung said.

“So this time will be different. Mount Hua is no longer alone. Even if not as strong as before, there are those who will fight alongside and protect each other. So don’t be afraid. Because you can do it.”

Everyone nodded. Though clumsy, these words resonated with them much more.

“Right. If Mount Hua was alone before, it couldn’t have done anything within that alliance.”

“But now it’s not. The Sichuan Tang family will do their best to help us.”

“Not just the Sichuan family. So even if we form an alliance, we don’t need to bow our heads. Mount Hua is no longer just a single sect, but a representative of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

Baek Cheon sighed deeply.

‘The homework is piling up.’

In the past, Chung Myung would have explained in detail so everyone could understand what he said, even if annoyed. Isn’t that how Chung Myung is?

But this time was different. He just dropped the topic and stepped back, as if telling them not to follow blindly but to think for themselves.

“Everyone will have different interpretations.”

“...”

“But I think that’s right. After all, an alliance is a place where people with different thoughts coordinate those thoughts.”

“Yes, sasuk.”

“I think so. What Samae said is right, and what the Green Forest King said also... has some chance of being right.”

“...But the treatment seems too different in acceptance.”

Baek Cheon ignored Im So-Byeong’s words and spoke.

“But I think this: the core of the conversation between Jang Ilso and Chung Myung is just the Heavenly Friends Alliance itself. The Heavenly Friends Alliance was created for mutual protection. Such a place should not force sacrifices on each other in the name of protecting the murim world. That would fundamentally go against why we created the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“...That’s a correct statement.”

“It will be a hard path. Chung Myung says that he must protect the realm from the Demonic Cult’s claws and protect everyone he wants to protect. It will be a thorny path. So...”

Baek Cheon raised his head and looked at Chung Myung again.

The full moon above softly illuminated Chung Myung.

“Let’s help him. We are also those who want to protect him.”

“Yes, sasuk.”

“Yes, sahyung.”

A gentle smile appeared on Baek Cheon’s lips as he looked at Chung Myung.

Everyone doesn’t need to think the same. What’s important is to trust each other and try to protect each other.

That is the alliance, that is the league, that is the sect.

Yes. That is family.

“Kuuuuuh...”

Chung Myung twisted his body mournfully on the eaves, goosebumps like tiny grains crawling down his back.

“Why did I say something like that...”

“Kyaaaak! Shut up! Did I say something wrong?”

“Ugh.”

Chung Myung sighed deeply.

“I did overdo it a bit.”

He had been carried away by the mood—something that could never have happened in the past.

Isn’t it ridiculous when you think about it? To dare compare those chick-like fools to the great ‘Great Mount Hua Sect.’ If the old Chung disciples who treated Shaolin as a ‘hopeless bald-headed cult revival meeting’ had heard this, they’d have their eyes pop out and go wild, saying they’d beat up descendants and all.

“...Come to think of it, those bastards were hopeless.”

That’s why the entire Central Plains turned their backs. Crazy bastards.

“You’re especially talkative today. Ah, if you’re annoyed, why don’t you come back to life and see?”

Chung Myung snorted and scoffed, then flopped back down on the eaves.

“...It’s not exactly wrong.”

If the Plum Blossom Sword Saint and the current Chung Myung were not the same person?

Probably the person who would have bitten his sword and run at the front to correct such insolence would have been none other than Chung Myung himself. He might even have knocked out that guy Chung Jin who wanted to leave it be and carried him under his arm while running.

“...I was hot-headed too... I was young, very young.”

The old Chung Myung did not properly see Mount Hua of his time.

The murim world is a place where the capable are respected, and Mount Hua was more capable than any other sect in its time. So, Chung Myung thought the incompetent should just shut up and follow orders.

From that perspective, Mount Hua of that era was definitely a sect worthy of that. It was stronger and more outstanding than any other sect. But...

‘That wasn’t all there was to it.’

Chung Myung scratched his head.

If you think about it, Chung Myung had never once thought from the perspective of the weak. Mount Hua, which ruled the era, and even within it, Chung Myung was in an overwhelmingly strong position.

Even when he was young, wasn’t Chung Myung specially treated?

So he thought it was natural.

The kindness Chung Myung showed as a disciple of Mount Hua did not come from thinking about the weak. It was simply that the strong should naturally do so, and he acted on that without doubt.

But after falling to what could be called rock bottom and struggling to climb back up, he realized that the world is not just about strength and weakness.

The leaders are the strong, but the weak also have their own will and thoughts. Just because they are weak doesn’t mean their ways should be ignored or belittled.

So to sum up...

‘If I saw the old Plum Blossom Sword Saint now, I’d have beaten him to death.’

Well, considering the difference in skill and temperament, rather than beating him to death, Chung Myung would probably be the one getting beaten to near death. Either way, he wouldn’t have looked on him kindly. Looking back, it’s hard to find someone who lived a more messed-up life than that bastard.

So how unfair it must have been for those who got beaten by Chung Myung back then. A bastard no better than a scoundrel beating up anyone who annoyed him, and at that time, Mount Hua, the most gangster-like sect, so they couldn’t even protest...

To give an example, it’s like Hae Yeon running wild in Shaolin like the old Chung Myung. If Hae Yeon had done that, what would Chung Myung have done?

‘He probably would have beaten Hae Yeon until hair grew on his bald head.’

But... unfortunately, at that time, there was no place capable of handling the Plum Blossom Sword Saint and Mount Hua. So they had no choice but to endure with tears in their eyes.

Looking back, a surge of regret wells up...

“Ah, gotta exclude those Southern Edge bastards!”

Those bastards deserve even more beating! Absolutely, no doubt.

Clenching his fist, Chung Myung sighed deeply once again.

If you accept the logic that the strong should lead and can do whatever they want, then the actions of those Demonic Cult bastards must be justified to some extent.

If you don’t accept that, then naturally your thoughts about Mount Hua in the past must also change.

“Thinking about it pisses me off.”

Chung Myung turned his head with a baffled expression and looked somewhere.

“From those bastards’ old perspective, we’re no different from those Shaolin bastards now.”

“Ah, be quiet. What a sinner you are.”

Chung Myung pointed accusingly at the distant sky.

Of course, from Chung Myung’s perspective, it might be an unfair story. Mount Hua was a sect that definitely took responsibility for those above. Mount Hua shed the most blood wherever and whenever, and fought the fiercest battles.

It is not a sect that can be compared to the current Shaolin, who stand one step behind with their hands behind their backs, just gesturing.

But conversely...

“So now, if those Shaolin bastards fight hard, will they look on them favorably?”

“See?”

Chung Myung scoffed. Others might not understand Shaolin, but honestly, Chung Myung somewhat understands why Shaolin acts that way.

From Shaolin’s perspective, they have the deepest history, the strongest forces, and the most masters, so they believe they should lead the murim world.

They have deep thoughts behind their actions, so it must be absurd to them that sects not even half as big as Shaolin keep criticizing them for having different ideas.

From Shaolin’s point of view, the Namgung bastards ignored orders to stay put and recklessly charged Plum Blossom Island, nearly facing annihilation, making them the dumbest sect in the world...

“Huh? Isn’t that right?”

Chung Myung glanced down and looked at Namgung Do-Wei who was far away.

No... to be more cold-hearted, it’s like Namgung Hwang took everything and jumped into the Yangtze River. But to curse Shaolin over this is a bit over the line...

“Kuh, ahem. Anyway.”

And from Shaolin’s viewpoint, Mount Hua and Tang Family are stirring trouble in the well-united Central Plains, bringing in foreign forces, making them crazy sects.

Even though the Evil Sect openly runs rampant, instead of joining forces, they act like arrogant bastards saying, ‘If you’re frustrated, crawl under us.’ Even when the leader personally came to bow.

“Wow...”

Chung Myung looked up at the night sky with trembling eyes.

“When you reverse it, everything makes sense, Chung Mun sahyung?”

“Who said anything? Don’t feel guilty for no reason.”

Chung Myung chuckled.

Chung Myung knows that all of Shaolin’s actions can be interpreted with the three words ‘superiority complex.’ Shaolin will surely fight bravely at the front once again when they reclaim their position as the representative of the murim world, just like Mount Hua did in the past.

What they want is not mere profit but the status of ‘Shaolin,’ the sect that protects the martial world. They know that this title cannot be gained without shedding blood.

But...

“That’s meaningless, you fool.”

Now Chung Myung knows. He has already experienced it. That it means nothing. Such actions leave nothing behind. That they end up on the same path as countless sects that once ruled the world but are now forgotten.

“Chung Mun sahyung, I...”

Chung Myung gazed at the night sky.

Countless stars looked down on him, as if his past sworn brothers were watching over him.

“I liked Mount Hua.”

To be precise, he longed for the old Mount Hua. So he wanted to make the current Mount Hua like that old Mount Hua—a sect superior to any other, more excellent than any other.

“But... that was just my selfish desire.”

He now knows that it was the wrong path.

They have already failed once. They tasted such a bitter failure that they can’t even imagine a bigger one.

Yet if they repeat the same mistakes, it’s no different from walking toward a predetermined failure. If you want to change the outcome, the process must also change.

Mount Hua, which stood out alone, failed. And Chung Myung now clearly sees what a sect trying to stand out alone is becoming.

So... now he and Mount Hua must change.

Now Chung Myung understands.

How a weak sect thinks and lives. What kind of heart a weak murim warrior holds. How much those who support the strong from behind are struggling.

Being weak doesn’t mean they deserve to be ignored. Even if lacking, their efforts and thoughts must be respected as they are.

Aren’t they there? Those who are proof of this.

Chung Myung turned his head and looked at Baek Cheon and his group. Their faces were quite serious, as if having a serious conversation. A smirk escaped Chung Myung’s lips.

‘What are these kids so serious about?’

What if they had joined the old Mount Hua? If they had entered as past Chung disciples like Chung Myung or the later Myung disciples?

Perhaps those kids would not have stood out, forgotten as mediocre disciples, or eventually failed to endure Mount Hua’s problems and left.

But now those kids have grown into proud members of Mount Hua and its future.

It’s the same. Being lacking doesn’t mean no potential. And being weak doesn’t mean no value.

“I should have known that a little earlier.”

Mount Hua of the past and Shaolin of now show that a strong but unaccepting sect is meaningless.

So the current Mount Hua must change.

It’s okay not to be as strong as before or to lead as firmly. There are many who can fill Mount Hua’s shortcomings.

Therefore, it’s not about leading alone but walking together.

‘Can I do it?’

Chung Myung quietly closed his eyes.

It’s easy to say, but it’s very difficult. It might be twice as hard as making the current Mount Hua as strong as the old one.

Chung Myung opened his eyes and looked at those gathered below.

Mount Hua and Shaolin, Namgung Family and Green Forest.

Those who seemed completely incompatible were raising their voices and bickering with each other.

Some might call that scene a mess, others might disparage it as a hodgepodge.

But Chung Myung’s thoughts were a bit different. If he had to name that scene...

“...”

A smile blossomed on Chung Myung’s lips.

“Yes. You’re right.”

He stood up and stretched.

“Things are going to get busy.”

It’s still a difficult task. The Demonic Cult is too powerful, the Justice faction is faltering. And a monster who prioritizes his own desires over the world’s peace is eyeing beyond the river. But...

“No need to worry. I’ll make it happen.”

Because now, I’m not ‘I’ but ‘we.’

Chung Myung grinned, tightly gripped the bottle in his hand, and jumped down from the eaves.

“Stop fighting, you bastards!”

“What did you say?”

“We’re not fighting, you know?”

Chung Myung slipped through the noisy crowd.

Their loud conversation continued long past when the moon had risen high.

“That’s a good thing to say. Yes, it’s a good thing.”

Chung Myung, lying back on the bench holding a bottle of alcohol, muttered indifferently.

“Going together, matching steps. Of course, those are good words. But...”

Glug glug glug.

The alcohol went down his throat smoothly.

“Kahaaa! This is killing me!”

He pulled the bottle from his mouth vigorously and turned it around in his hand.

“Is it because the water tastes good here? The alcohol goes down so smoothly.”

Chung Myung, licking his lips happily, soon returned to his indifferent expression and said.

“No, but think about it. No matter how much you try to match steps, can a person match steps with a grub? No, right?”

“......”

“So at least you have to do that. At least. Huh? At least treat them like a person so you can match steps or hands, right? No matter how you look at it, they’re not human, so how can I possibly lead them?”

Chung Myung slightly turned his head and asked.

“Isn’t that right?”

“......”

“I said, isn’t that right?”

“Ughhh...”

But what came back was not an answer but a dying groan.

Baek Cheon, covered in dirt and lying face down, glared at Chung Myung with eyes full of deadly poison.

Glug glug glug glug.

But Chung Myung didn’t even glance at him and just kept drinking vigorously.

“Kruuu!”

Chung Myung pulled the bottle from his mouth and clicked his tongue with a long sigh.

“So at least you have to make them human. Only then can we try to do anything.”

“...Son of a bitch.”

“What do ghosts... what do ghosts even do in life... not taking that bastard away.”

“No, now I just wish they’d take me away. This place is hell... anywhere without that bastard is heaven to me.”

It was when Five Swords, even more battered than when they fought in Hangzhou, was spitting out venomous curses.

Behind them, Im So-Byeong, who was in the same sorry state... no, even more pitiful in appearance, opened his mouth with a dying voice.

“No... I, I... no, I... why...”

Five Swords looked back at Im So-Byeong with indifferent eyes.

“Didn’t you hear?”

“What...”

“Now the sects belonging to the Heavenly Friends Alliance move forward together with Mount Hua.”

“But that...”

Yu Yiseol said with a sullen face.

“Because we’re friends. Equal treatment. Equal training.”

“......”

“So the Green Forest Sect will roll along too. Just like Mount Hua.”

“I... I’m a patient...”

“Even patients roll along together.”

“Is that even something a human says?”

The ones protesting in dying voices were not the Green Forest Sect members, but the Tang family members rolling alongside them.

Tears welled up in the eyes of those twisting their bodies in rage.

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, they’d only heard of it. No, they’d seen it too, but they thought that man’s rampage had nothing to do with the Tang family.

Who in their right mind storms in at dawn and shouts, ‘From today, I’ll take charge of your training. If you feel like you are unfairly treated, go complain to the Tang family lord’?

And what is Tang Gunak doing that he hasn’t even shown his face since morning to stop this?

Resistance?

Of course, they tried. It was more like timid resistance than real rebellion.

But after Tang Zhan, the one who tried to reason with Chung Myung nicely, was kicked and thrown into the icy Yangtze River, talk of resistance or rebellion became irrelevant to them.

“Um...”

At that moment, Namgung Do-Wei, who was sprawled out, quietly raised his hand.

“What now?”

“May I ask a question?”

“Go ahead.”

Namgung Do-Wei glanced around cautiously before speaking.

“Um... earlier you gathered us and said... now we go together, right?”

“That’s right.”

“But what exactly does that have to do with this training... Ah, of course, our Namgung Family is doing what we’ve always done, so no complaints, but I think the Tang family and Green Forest folks have different circumstances.”

“Ah, all this?”

Chung Myung hummed thoughtfully for a moment and then nodded.

“If you want an explanation, I should give you.”

At those words, Five Swords’s eyes widened.

“An explanation... from him?”

“That bastard?”

“Did the sun rise in the west?”

It was a perfectly reasonable reaction. Chung Myung only explains when he feels like it; otherwise, he just ignores you.

If you were to guess Chung Myung’s proper reaction here, it would be, ‘If I tell you to dig, you’ll dig. Who is this wet-behind-the-ears kid glaring and pestering me?’ But would he really explain so obediently?

At that moment of confusion, Tang Soso quietly muttered.

“They paid a lot of money.”

“Ah...”

Five Swords nodded in understanding at that one sentence.

Yeah. They did pay a lot. No money, crime; money, no crime. This dirty world...

Regardless, Chung Myung shrugged and continued.

“I’ve realized it.”

“...Yes? What do you mean?”

Chung Myung slammed the bottle violently onto the bench. His eyes blazed fiercely.

“Those Demonic cult bastards are so bloodshot, waiting for the Heavenly Demon to return! That sly Jang Ilso is licking his lips trying to do something about the Northern lands!”

“......”

“I was only obsessed with Mount Hua, that’s what I’m saying.”

Chung Myung’s eyes gleamed as he looked over those groaning in front of him.

“There are so many ‘ingredients’ here.”

At a glance, it looked like a fire was burning in Chung Myung’s eyes. Maybe it really was.

“What does going together mean?”

“...Being of one mind and heart...”

“That bastard! You can tell he’s from a good family by the obvious nonsense he’s spouting again.”

Bang!

Chung Myung slammed his palm hard on the bench and glared.

“Going together means sharing responsibility! It’s joint responsibility!”

“......”

“In the past, only Mount Hua took the blame. But thinking about it, there was no reason for that! Is it Mount Hua’s fault for not stopping the Demonic Cult bastards? You have responsibility too.”

“Ah, no...”

That’s true, but... well...

Chung Myung, who was angry, suddenly smirked.

“We have to go together. Yes, we must. But why should only Mount Hua suffer? Everyone should equally see hell, equally visit their deceased grandfathers briefly, and share the burden equally.”

“Ah, no. That’s...!”

“My grandfather is still alive...”

Just as the Green Forest and Tang family members tried to protest.

Clap clap clap clap clap.

Loud applause came from beside them.

The Mount Hua guys, who were dying, suddenly regained their energy, stood up, and gave Chung Myung a loud round of applause.

Then, wiping tears that hadn’t even come out, they repeatedly praised him with wet voices.

“That was an inspiring speech.”

“I’m usually not one to cry, but this made me tear up.”

“I agree!”

Those bastards...
